Proof of Theorem
4.1
.

According to Lemma
6.1
, Lemma
6.2
and Lemma
6.3
, and take
\omega_{\mu}=\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime}=\frac{1}{T^{2}}
and
\omega_{\mathrm{v}}=\frac{\delta_{T}}{T^{2}}
, the expected regret of
T^{\prime}
phases
\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{R}(T^{\prime})]
can be upper bounded as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{R}(T^{\prime})]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{R}_{1}(T^{\prime})|\mathcal{E}]+\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{R}_{2}(T^{\prime})|\mathcal{E}]+\mathrm{R}_{3}(T)

(330)

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\sum_{i\in E\setminus S^{\star}}\frac{K}{\Delta_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B},\min}}\ln\frac{1}{\omega_{\mu}}+\sum_{i\in E}\frac{c_{i}K}{\Delta_{i,\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B},\min}}\ln\frac{1}{\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime}}\right)+2\mu^{\star}\left[T^{\prime}\cdot(r^{\prime}-1)+H(r^{\prime},\Lambda)\right]

(331)

\displaystyle\quad+2\mu^{\star}L+2\mu^{\star}TL\left(\xi(\omega_{\mu})+2\xi(\omega_{v})+2\xi(\omega_{v}^{\prime})\right)

(332)

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\sum_{i\in E\setminus S^{\star}}\frac{K}{\Delta_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B},\min}}\ln T+\sum_{i\in E}\frac{c_{i}K}{\Delta_{i,\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B},\min}}\ln T\right)+2\mu^{\star}H(1,\Lambda)

(333)

\displaystyle\quad+2\mu^{\star}L+2\mu^{\star}TL\left(3\xi(1/T^{2})+2\xi(\delta_{T}/T^{2})\right)

(334)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Reg}_{\mathrm{1}}(T)+\mathrm{Reg}_{\mathrm{2}}(T)+\mathrm{Reg}_{\mathrm{3}}(T)

(335)

On the other hand, the high-probability regret (
112
) can be naïve bounded as

\displaystyle\hat{\mathrm{R}}(T^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=1}^{T^{\prime}}\sum_{r=1}^{n_{p}}(\mu^{\star}-\mu_{A_{p,r}})

(336)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{p=1}^{T^{\prime}}\sum_{r=1}^{n_{p}}\mu^{\star}

(337)

\displaystyle=T\mu^{\star}.

(338)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{R}(T^{\prime})]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[\hat{\mathrm{R}}(T^{\prime})|\mathcal{E}]+\mathrm{Reg}_{\mathrm{3}}(T)

(339)

\displaystyle\leq T\mu^{\star}+\mathrm{Reg}_{\mathrm{3}}(T)

(340)

(
330
) and (
339
) give the final upper bound with
T
time steps.

See
5.1

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

We firstly deal with the regret due to suboptimality
\mathrm{R}_{1}(T^{\prime})
.
Let
\Delta^{\mu}
be a constant that is to be chosen.

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}_{1}(T^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=1}^{T^{\prime}}\mathbbm{1}\{A_{p}\in\mathcal{B}\}\Delta_{A_{p}}

(341)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=1}^{T^{\prime}}\mathbbm{1}\{\Delta_{A_{p}}\geq\Delta^{\mu}\}\mathbbm{1}\{A_{p}\in\mathcal{B}\}\Delta_{A_{p}}+\sum_{p=1}^{T^{\prime}}\mathbbm{1}\{\Delta_{A_{p}}<\Delta^{\mu}\}\mathbbm{1}\{A_{p}\in\mathcal{B}\}\Delta_{A_{p}}

(342)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{p=1}^{T^{\prime}}\mathbbm{1}\{\Delta_{A_{p}}\geq\Delta^{\mu}\}\mathbbm{1}\{A_{p}\in\mathcal{B}\}\Delta_{A_{p}}+T\cdot\Delta^{\mu}

(343)

where the second term makes use of the fact that
T^{\prime}\leq T
w.p.
1
.

The first term can be upper bounded by adopting the proof of Lemma
6.3
with the constraint that
\Delta_{A_{p}}\geq\Delta^{\mu}
. Thus,
for
i\in E\setminus S^{\star}
,

\Delta_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B},\min}\geq\Delta^{\mu}.

For
i\in E
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{i,\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B},\min}

\displaystyle\geq\Delta^{\mu}

(344)

\displaystyle\bar{\Delta}_{i,\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\geq\max\{\bar{\omega}\Delta^{\mu},\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}/3\}=\max\{\Delta^{\mu},\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}/3\}=:\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}}

(345)

\displaystyle c_{i}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\bar{\omega}^{2}}=1

(346)

where
\bar{\omega}:=\sqrt{\frac{\ln\frac{1}{\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime}}}{\ln\frac{1}{\omega_{\mu}}}}=1
.
The regret due to suboptimality can be upper bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{T^{\prime}}\mathbbm{1}\{\Delta_{A_{p}}\geq\Delta^{\mu}\}\mathbbm{1}\{A_{p}\in\mathcal{B}\}\Delta_{A_{p}}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in E\setminus S^{\star}}\frac{2C\gamma K}{\Delta_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B},\min}}\left(2\ln\frac{1}{\omega_{\mu}}+\ln\ln_{+}\frac{1}{\Delta_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B},\min}^{2}}+D\right)

(347)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i\in E}\frac{2c_{i}\cdot C\gamma K}{\Delta_{i,\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B},\min}}\left(2\ln\frac{1}{\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime}}+\ln\ln_{+}\frac{1}{(\bar{\Delta}_{i,\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}}^{\prime})^{2}}+D\right)

(348)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in E\setminus S^{\star}}\frac{2C\gamma K}{\Delta^{\mu}}\left(2\ln\frac{1}{\omega_{\mu}}+\ln\ln_{+}\frac{1}{(\Delta^{\mu})^{2}}+D\right)

(349)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i\in E}\frac{2C\gamma K}{\Delta^{\mu}}\left(2\ln\frac{1}{\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime}}+\ln\ln_{+}\frac{1}{(\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}})^{2}}+D\right)

(350)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle L\cdot\frac{8C\gamma K}{\Delta^{\mu}}\left(2\ln T+\ln\ln_{+}\frac{1}{(\Delta^{\mu})^{2}}+D\right)

(351)

By taking
\Delta^{\mu}=\sqrt{\frac{KL\ln(TL)}{T}}
, the regret due to suboptimality is bounded by

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}_{1}(T^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{p=1}^{T^{\prime}}\mathbbm{1}\{\Delta_{A_{p}}\geq\Delta^{\mu}\}\mathbbm{1}\{A_{p}\in\mathcal{B}\}\Delta_{A_{p}}+T\cdot\Delta^{\mu}

(352)

\displaystyle\leq 16C\gamma\sqrt{KLT\ln(TL)}+8C\gamma\sqrt{\frac{KLT}{\ln T}}\ln\ln_{+}\frac{T}{KL\ln T}+8C\gamma\sqrt{\frac{KLT}{\ln T}}D+\sqrt{KLT\ln(TL)}

(353)

\displaystyle=O(\sqrt{KLT\ln(TL)})

(354)

\displaystyle=O(\sqrt{KLT\ln T}).

(355)

where we utilize
T\geq L
.

We then cope with the regret due to safeness-checking
\mathrm{R}_{2}(T^{\prime}).
According to Lemma
6.3
, we only need to upper bound
H(1,\Lambda)
, i.e., the
h
functions defined in (
321
), (
324
), (
326
) and (
328
).

For
h_{S^{\star}}(1,\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{S^{\star}})
:

\displaystyle h_{S^{\star}}(1,\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{S^{\star}})

\displaystyle\leq h_{S^{\star}}(1,\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})

(356)

\displaystyle=\frac{18\cdot C\gamma K}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\left(2\ln\frac{1}{\omega_{\mathrm{v}}}+\ln\ln_{+}\frac{1}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}+D\right)

(357)

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{K}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln\frac{1}{\omega_{\mathrm{v}}}\right)

(358)

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{K}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln\frac{T}{\delta}\right)

(359)

For
h_{\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}}(1,\bar{\Delta}_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}})
, define

\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}}:=\max\left\{\frac{\Delta^{\mu}}{\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mu})}},\frac{\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}}{3\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}})}}\right\}=\max\left\{\sqrt{\frac{KL}{T}},\frac{\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}}{3\sqrt{\ln(TL/\delta)}}\right\}
.
We have
\bar{\Delta}_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}}\geq\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}}
.
The threshold

\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}}}{\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{3\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}})}}}\right\rfloor=\left\lfloor\frac{\max\left\{3\sqrt{\frac{KL\ln(TL/\delta)}{T}},\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}\right\}}{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}\right\rfloor.

\displaystyle h_{\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}}(1,\bar{\Delta}_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}})\leq h_{\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}}(1,\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}})

(360)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{aligned} &(Q-1)\frac{C\gamma K}{(\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\left(2+\frac{1}{\ln(1/\omega_{\mu\mathrm{v}})}\left(\ln\ln_{+}\frac{1}{\ln(1/\omega_{\mu\mathrm{v}})(\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}})^{2}}+D\right)\right),\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}}}{\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{3\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}})}}}\right\rfloor\geq Q-3\\
&C\gamma K\frac{4}{\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{3\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}})}}\cdot\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}}}\left(2+\frac{1}{\ln(1/\omega_{\mu\mathrm{v}})}\left(\ln\ln_{+}\frac{1}{\ln(1/\omega_{\mu\mathrm{v}})(\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}})^{2}}+D\right)\right),0<\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}}}{\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{3\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}})}}}\right\rfloor<Q-3\\
&\frac{3\cdot C\gamma K}{(\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\left(2+\frac{1}{\ln(1/\omega_{\mu\mathrm{v}})}\left(\ln\ln_{+}\frac{1}{\ln(1/\omega_{\mu\mathrm{v}})(\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}})^{2}}+D\right)\right),\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}}}{\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{3\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}})}}}\right\rfloor=0\end{aligned}\right.

(361)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{aligned} &O\left((Q-1)\frac{K}{(\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\right),&&\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}}}{\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{3\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}})}}}\right\rfloor\geq Q-3\\
&O\left(\frac{K}{\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{3\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}})}}\cdot\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}}}\right),&&0<\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}}}{\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{3\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}})}}}\right\rfloor<Q-3\\
&O\left(\frac{K}{(\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\right),&&\left\lfloor\frac{\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}}}{\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{3\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}})}}}\right\rfloor=0\end{aligned}\right.

(362)

In particular, in the asymptotic case where
T\to\infty
and
\ln\frac{1}{\delta}=\ln\frac{1}{\delta_{T}}=o(T^{b}),\forall b>0
(this includes the scenario where
\delta
is fixed with respect to
T
), we have

\displaystyle h_{\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}}(1,\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}})=O\left(\frac{K}{(\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\right)=O\left(\frac{K}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln\frac{T}{\delta}\right)

(363)

For
h_{\mathcal{R}}(1,\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{i,\mathcal{R}})
, we have
\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{i,\mathcal{R}}\geq\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}
. Furthermore,
\omega_{\mathrm{sum}}=\sqrt{\ln(TL)}+\sqrt{\ln(TL/\delta)}
and the changing point
\left\lfloor\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{sum}}\cdot\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}}{\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime})}\bar{\sigma}^{2}}\right\rfloor=\left\lfloor\frac{(\sqrt{\ln(TL)}+\sqrt{\ln(TL/\delta)})\cdot\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}}{\sqrt{\ln(TL)}\bar{\sigma}^{2}}\right\rfloor
.

Thus

\displaystyle h_{\mathcal{R}}(1,\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{i,\mathcal{R}})\leq h_{\mathcal{R}}(r^{\prime},\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})

(364)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{aligned} &(Q-1)\frac{C\gamma K}{(\frac{\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}}{3\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime})}})^{2}}\left(2+\frac{1}{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}\mathrm{v}^{\prime}})}\left(\ln\ln_{+}\frac{1}{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}\mathrm{v}^{\prime}})(\frac{\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}}{3\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime})}})^{2}}+D\right)\right),\left\lfloor\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{sum}}\cdot\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}}{\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime})}\bar{\sigma}^{2}}\right\rfloor\geq Q-3\\
&C\gamma K\frac{3}{\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{3\omega_{\mathrm{sum}}}\cdot\frac{\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{i,\mathcal{R}}}{3\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime})}}}\left(2+\frac{1}{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}\mathrm{v}^{\prime}})}\left(\ln\ln_{+}\frac{1}{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}\mathrm{v}^{\prime}})(\frac{\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{i,\mathcal{R}}}{3\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime})}})^{2}}+D\right)\right),0<\left\lfloor\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{sum}}\cdot\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}}{\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime})}\bar{\sigma}^{2}}\right\rfloor\leq Q-3\\
&\frac{3C\gamma K}{(\frac{\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}}{3\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime})}})^{2}}\left(2+\frac{1}{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}\mathrm{v}^{\prime}})}\left(\ln\ln_{+}\frac{1}{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}\mathrm{v}^{\prime}})(\frac{\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}}{3\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime})}})^{2}}+D\right)\right),\left\lfloor\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{sum}}\cdot\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}}{\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime})}\bar{\sigma}^{2}}\right\rfloor=0\end{aligned}\right.

(365)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{aligned} &O\left((Q-1)\frac{K}{(\frac{\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}}{3\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime})}})^{2}}\right),&&\left\lfloor\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{sum}}\cdot\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}}{\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime})}\bar{\sigma}^{2}}\right\rfloor\geq Q-3\\
&O\left(\frac{K}{\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{3\omega_{\mathrm{sum}}}\cdot\frac{\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{i,\mathcal{R}}}{3\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime})}}}\right),&&0<\left\lfloor\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{sum}}\cdot\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}}{\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime})}\bar{\sigma}^{2}}\right\rfloor\leq Q-3\\
&=O\left(\frac{K}{(\frac{\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}}{3\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime})}})^{2}}\right),&&\left\lfloor\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{sum}}\cdot\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}}{\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime})}\bar{\sigma}^{2}}\right\rfloor=0\end{aligned}\right.

(366)

In particular, in the asymptotic case where
T\to\infty
and
\ln\frac{1}{\delta}=\ln\frac{1}{\delta_{T}}=o(T^{b}),\forall b>0
, we have

\displaystyle h_{\mathcal{R}}(1,\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})=O\left(\frac{QK}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln\frac{1}{\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime}}\right)=O\left(\frac{QK}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln T\right)

(367)

For
\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}
: define
\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}}:=\max\left\{\frac{\Delta^{\mu}}{\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mu})}},\frac{\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}}{3\sqrt{\ln(1/\omega_{\mathrm{v}}^{\prime})}}\right\}=\max\left\{\sqrt{\frac{KL}{T}},\frac{\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}}{3\sqrt{\ln(TL)}}\right\}.

We have

\bar{\Delta}_{i,\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}}\geq\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}}
and the changing point

\left\lfloor\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{sum}}\cdot\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}}}{\bar{\sigma}^{2}/3}\right\rfloor=\left\lfloor\frac{(\sqrt{\ln(TL)}+\sqrt{\ln(TL/\delta)})\cdot\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}}}{\bar{\sigma}^{2}/3}\right\rfloor

thus

\displaystyle h_{\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}}(1,\bar{\Delta}_{i,\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}})\leq h_{\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}}(1,\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}})

(368)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{aligned} &(Q-1)\frac{C\gamma K}{(\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\left(2+\frac{1}{\ln(1/\omega_{\max})}\left(\ln\ln_{+}\frac{1}{\ln(1/\omega_{\max})(\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}})^{2}}+D\right)\right),\left\lfloor\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{sum}}\cdot\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}}}{\bar{\sigma}^{2}/3}\right\rfloor\geq Q-3\\
&C\gamma K\left(\frac{3}{\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{3\omega_{\mathrm{sum}}}\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}}}\right)\left(2+\frac{1}{\ln(1/\omega_{\max})}\left(\ln\ln_{+}\frac{1}{\ln(1/\omega_{\max})(\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}})^{2}}+D\right)\right),0<\left\lfloor\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{sum}}\cdot\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}}}{\bar{\sigma}^{2}/3}\right\rfloor<Q-3\\
&\frac{3C\gamma K}{(\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\left(2+\frac{1}{\ln(1/\omega_{\max})}\left(\ln\ln_{+}\frac{1}{\ln(1/\omega_{\max})(\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}})^{2}}+D\right)\right),\left\lfloor\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{sum}}\cdot\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}}}{\bar{\sigma}^{2}/3}\right\rfloor=0,r^{\prime}=1\end{aligned}\right.

(369)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{aligned} &O\left((Q-1)\frac{K}{(\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\right),&&\left\lfloor\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{sum}}\cdot\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}}}{\bar{\sigma}^{2}/3}\right\rfloor\geq Q-3\\
&O\left(\frac{K}{\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{3\omega_{\mathrm{sum}}}\cdot\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}}}\right),&&0<\left\lfloor\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{sum}}\cdot\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}}}{\bar{\sigma}^{2}/3}\right\rfloor<Q-3\\
&O\left(\frac{K}{(\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\right),&&\left\lfloor\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{sum}}\cdot\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}}}{\bar{\sigma}^{2}/3}\right\rfloor=0,r^{\prime}=1\end{aligned}\right.

(370)

In particular, in the asymptotic case where
T\to\infty
and
\ln\frac{1}{\delta}=\ln\frac{1}{\delta_{T}}=o(T^{b}),\forall b>0
, we have

\displaystyle h_{\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}}(1,\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}})=O\left(\frac{QK}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln T\right)

(371)

Lastly,

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}_{3}(T)

\displaystyle=2\mu^{\star}L+2\mu^{\star}TL\left(\xi(\omega_{\mu})+2\xi(\omega_{v})+2\xi(\omega_{v}^{\prime})\right)

(372)

\displaystyle\leq 2KL+K\delta+2KTL\cdot 4\cdot\frac{2+\epsilon}{\epsilon}\big(\frac{\frac{1}{T^{2}}}{\ln(1+\epsilon)}\big)^{1+\epsilon}

(373)

\displaystyle\leq 2KL+K\delta+4K\cdot\frac{2+\epsilon}{\epsilon}\big(\frac{1}{\ln(1+\epsilon)}\big)^{1+\epsilon}

(374)

\displaystyle=O(1)

(375)

where we utilize
T>L
and the
O
notation refers to the fact that the preceding term is bounded as a function of
T
.

Note that
\mu^{\star}\leq K
, so
T\mu^{\star}+K\delta\leq TK+K\delta
.

In conclusion
, according to Theorem
4.1
, for any
T>L
, the problem-independent upper bound is the minimum of
TK+K\delta
and

\displaystyle O(\sqrt{KLT\ln T})+K\left(\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}h_{S^{\star}}(1,\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})+\sum_{i\in E}h_{\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}}(1,\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}})+\sum_{i\in E}h_{\mathcal{R}}(1,\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})+\sum_{i\in E}h_{\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}}(1,\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}})\right)

(376)

\displaystyle\leq O(\sqrt{KLT\ln T})+K\left(Kh_{S^{\star}}(1,\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})+Lh_{\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}}(1,\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}})+Lh_{\mathcal{R}}(1,\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})+Lh_{\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}}(1,\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}})\right)

(377)

\displaystyle=O(\sqrt{KLT\ln T})+O\left(\frac{K^{3}}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln\frac{T}{\delta}\right)+KL\left(h_{\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}}(1,\Delta^{\mu\mathrm{v}})+h_{\mathcal{R}}(1,\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})+h_{\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}}(1,\bar{\Delta}^{\mu\mathrm{v}})\right)

(378)

where the
h
functions are defined in (
360
), (
364
) and (
368
).
In the asymptotic case where
T\to\infty
and
\ln\frac{1}{\delta}=\ln\frac{1}{\delta_{T}}=o(T^{b}),\forall b>0
(this includes the scenario where
\delta
is fixed with respect to
T
), the asymptotic problem-independent upper bound is

\displaystyle O(\sqrt{KLT\ln T})+K\left(\sum_{i\in E}O\left(\frac{K}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln\frac{T}{\delta}\right)+O\sum_{i\in E}\left(\frac{QK}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln T\right)\right)

(379)

\displaystyle=O(\sqrt{KLT\ln T})+O\left(\frac{LK^{2}}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln\frac{1}{\delta}\right)

(380)

where we utilize
\sqrt{T\ln T}\geq\frac{QK^{2}}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln T
when
T
is sufficiently large.
∎

Appendix C

Proofs of the Lower Bounds

C.1

Preliminaries and the Impossibility Result

Let
\mathrm{KL}(\nu,\nu^{\prime})
denote the KL divergence between distributions
\nu
and
\nu^{\prime}
, and

\displaystyle d(x,y):=x\ln\bigg(\frac{x}{y}\bigg)+(1-x)\ln\bigg(\frac{1-x}{1-y}\bigg)

denote the Kullback–Leibler (KL) divergence between the Bernoulli distributions
\mathrm{Bern}(x)
and
\mathrm{Bern}(y)
.

Lemma C.1

(Pinsker’s and reverse Pinsker’s Inequality)
.

Consider two probability mass functions
P_{X},P_{Y}
defined on the same discrete probability space
{\cal A}\subset[0,1]
. The following inequalities hold:

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}_{X\sim P_{X}}[X]-\mathbb{E}_{Y\sim P_{Y}}[Y]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\delta(P_{X},P_{Y})\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{KL}(P_{X},P_{Y})}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha_{Y}}}\cdot\delta(P_{X},P_{Y}),.

where

\delta(P_{X},P_{Y}):=\sup_{A\subseteq{\cal A}}\left\{\sum_{a\in A}P_{X}(a)-\sum_{a\in A}P_{Y}(a)\right\}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}|P_{X}(a)-P_{Y}(a)|

is the total variational distance,
and

\alpha_{Y}:=\min_{a\in\mathcal{A}:P_{Y}(a)>0}Q(a)\vphantom{\bigg(}
.

Lemma C.2

(Lemma 1 in
Kaufmann et al. 2016
)
.

Assume the distributions under instance
\Lambda_{1}=(E,\mathcal{A}_{K},\nu^{(1)},\bar{\sigma}^{2})
and instance
\Lambda_{2}=(E,\mathcal{A}_{K},\nu^{(2)},\bar{\sigma}^{2})
are mutually absolutely continuous. Given time budget
T
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}_{\Lambda_{1}}[N_{i}(T)]\cdot\mathrm{KL}(\nu_{i}^{(1)},\nu_{i}^{(2)})\geq\sup_{\mathcal{E}\in\mathcal{H}_{T}^{\Lambda_{1}}}d\big(\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{1}}(\mathcal{E}),~\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{2}}(\mathcal{E})\big).

where
N_{i}(t)
denotes the number of time steps item
i
is selected up to and including time step
t
and
\mathcal{H}_{T}^{\Lambda_{1}}
is all the possible events generated by instance
\Lambda_{1}
and algorithm
\pi
with
T
time steps.

Lemma C.3
.

Let solution
S
containing
|S|=m(q<m\leq K)
items be a safe solution under instance
\Lambda_{1}=(E,\mathcal{A}_{K},\nu^{(1)},\bar{\sigma}^{2})
. Each item in
S
is i.i.d. with reward distribution
\nu_{1}
, mean
\mu_{1}
and variance
\sigma_{1}^{2}<\bar{\sigma}^{2}
.
Define event

\mathcal{E}_{(t,1)}=\{S\text{ is identified as safe after time step $t$}\}
,

\mathcal{E}_{(t,2)}=\{S\text{ is chosen at least once after time step $t$}\}
,
and
\mathcal{E}_{(t)}=\mathcal{E}_{(t,1)}\cap\mathcal{E}_{(t,2)}
.
Assume there exists
\tau\leq T
such that
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{1}}[\mathcal{E}_{(\tau)}]\geq 1-\delta
and
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{1}}[\mathcal{E}_{(\tau-1,1)}]<1-\delta
.
If
\tau
exists, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in S}\mathbb{E}_{\Lambda_{1}}[N_{i}(\tau)]\geq\sup_{\nu_{2}\in E(\nu_{1})}\frac{d(\delta,1-\delta)}{\mathrm{KL}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})}.

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\Lambda_{1}}[M(\tau)]\geq\sup_{\nu_{2}\in E(\nu_{1})}\frac{1}{|S|-1}\cdot\frac{d(\delta,1-\delta)}{\mathrm{KL}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})}:=T(\nu^{(1)}),

where

M(t)
is the number of times that a solution
S^{\prime}\subset S
is sampled up to and include time step
t
and

E(\nu_{1})=\{\nu_{2}:\text{ the variance associated to }\nu_{2}\text{ is larger than }\bar{\sigma}^{2}/|S|\}
.

Proof.

With
\sigma_{2}^{2}>\bar{\sigma}^{2}/|S|
, we construct an alternative instance
\Lambda_{2}=(E,\mathcal{A}_{K},\nu_{2},\bar{\sigma}^{2})
, under which each item in
S
is with reward distribution
\nu_{2}
, mean
\mu_{2}
and variance
\sigma_{2}^{2}
, while the distributions of other items remain unchanged.

Define event

\mathcal{E}_{(t,1)}=\{S\text{ is identified as safe after time step $t$}\}
,

\mathcal{E}_{(t,2)}=\{S\text{ is chosen at least once after time step $t$}\}
,
and
\mathcal{E}_{(t)}=\mathcal{E}_{(t,1)}\cap\mathcal{E}_{(t,2)}
.
Assume there exists
\tau\leq T
such that
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{1}}[\mathcal{E}_{(\tau)}]\geq 1-\delta
and
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{1}}[\mathcal{E}_{(\tau-1,1)}]<1-\delta
.
Since
S
is unsafe under instance
\Lambda_{2}
and all the solutions chosen
\{S_{t}\}_{t=1}^{T}\subset\mathcal{A}_{K}
are safe with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{2}}[\mathcal{E}_{(t)}]<\delta
for all
t\leq T
.

We now apply Lemma
C.2
to obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in S}\mathbb{E}_{\Lambda_{1}}[N_{i}(\tau)]\cdot\mathrm{KL}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})\geq d(\mathbb{P}_{1}(\mathcal{E}_{(\tau)}^{c}),\mathbb{P}_{2}(\mathcal{E}_{(\tau)}^{c}))\geq d(\delta,1-\delta)~\Rightarrow~\sum_{i\in S}\mathbb{E}_{\Lambda_{1}}[N_{i}(\tau)]\geq\frac{d(\delta,1-\delta)}{\mathrm{KL}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})}.

Since
\mathbb{P}_{\Lambda_{1}}[\mathcal{E}_{(\tau-1,1)}]<1-\delta
, we can select at most
m-1
items at one time step among the first
\tau
time steps. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\Lambda_{1}}[M(\tau)]\geq\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i\in P}\mathbb{E}_{\Lambda_{1}}[N_{i}(\tau)]\geq\frac{1}{m-1}\cdot\frac{d(\delta,1-\delta)}{\mathrm{KL}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})}.

∎

See
4.5

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proof of Lemma
C.3
. We consider an alternative instance
\Lambda_{2}=(E,\mathcal{A}_{K},\nu^{(2)},\bar{\sigma}^{2})
with the distributions of the items in the optimal safe solution
S^{\star}
changed such that (assume the variances of all the items in
S^{\star}
are changed (increased))

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}(\sigma_{i}^{(2)})^{2}\geq\bar{\sigma}^{2}

(381)

i.e., under instance
\Lambda_{2}
, this solution
S^{\star}
is unsafe (thus not optimal safe). The other items remain unchanged.

By a similar argument as the proof of Lemma
C.3
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\mathbb{E}_{\Lambda_{1}}[N_{i}(\tau)]\cdot\mathrm{KL}(\nu_{i}^{(1)},\nu_{i}^{(2)})\geq d(\delta,1-\delta)

(382)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\mathbb{E}_{\Lambda_{1}}[N_{i}(\tau)]\geq\frac{d(\delta,1-\delta)}{\min_{i\in S^{\star}}\mathrm{KL}(\nu_{i}^{(1)},\nu_{i}^{(2)})}

(383)

So the safeness checking of
S^{\star}
will take
\Omega(\ln\frac{1}{2.4\delta_{T}})=\Omega(T^{b})

Recall the probably anytime-safe constraint (
3
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[\forall\,t\in[T],S_{t}\in\mathcal{S}\big]\geq 1-\delta_{T}.

(384)

This indicates at time step
t
, for any solution
S
, if
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(0)}}[S\in\mathcal{S}]<1-\delta_{T}
,
S
will not be selected at this time step. Otherwise, (
3
) is violated. Therefore, before the safeness of the optimal safe solution
S
is ascertained, it is not going to be sampled and the instantaneous regret will be lower bounded by
\min_{S\in\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}}\Delta_{S}
.

In conclusion, the regret is at least
\ln\frac{1}{2.4\delta_{T}}\cdot\min_{S\in\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}}\Delta_{S}=\Omega(T^{b})
.
∎

We derive both the problem-dependent and problem independent lower bounds on the
K
-path semi-bandit
problem. The items in the ground set are divided into
L_{0}
paths:
P_{1},\ldots,P_{L_{0}}
, each of which contains
K
unique items. Path
P_{j}
contains items
(j-1)K+1,\ldots,jK
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
L/K
is an integer.
A set
S
is a solution if and only if
S\subset P_{j}
for some
j
. In other words, the solution set
\mathcal{A}_{K}=\{S:S\subset P_{j},\ \exists j=1,\ldots,L_{0}\}
.
We let
N_{i}(t)
denote the number of time steps item
i
is selected up to and including time step
t
,
M_{j}(t)
denote the number of time steps a safe subset in path
j
is selected up to and including time step
t
, and
S_{j}(t)
denote the time steps when a safe subset in path
j
is selected, i.e.,

\displaystyle N_{i}(t)=\sum_{s=1}^{t}\mathbbm{1}\{i\in S_{s}\},\quad M_{j}(t)=\sum_{s=1}^{t}\mathbbm{1}\{S_{s}\subset P_{j},S_{s}\text{ is safe }\},\quad S_{j}(t)=\{1\leq s\leq t:S_{s}\subset P_{j},S_{s}\text{ is safe }\}.

We let
\mathrm{Reg}[j](t)
denote the regret accumulated in
S_{j}(t)
. Since all the chosen solutions are safe with probability at
least
1-\delta
, we have
\mathrm{Reg}(T)\geq(1-\delta)\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{L_{0}}\mathrm{Reg}[j](T)
.
In the following, we lower bound the regret accumulated in time steps where safe solutions are chosen.

We will construct several instances to prove each of the lower bounds (which will be specified in the proof) such that
under instance
k
(
\Lambda_{k}=(E,\mathcal{A}_{K},\nu^{(k)},\bar{\sigma}^{2})
),
the stochastic reward of items in path
j
(
1\leq j\leq L_{0}
) are
i.i.d.
, i.e. ,

\nu_{i}^{(k)}=\nu_{P_{j}}^{(k)},\forall i\in P_{j}
,
which will be specified in each case.
Under instance
k
, we define several other notations as follows:

•

Let
W_{i}(t)^{(k)}
be the random reward of arm
i
at time step
t
.

•

Let
S_{t}^{(k)}
be the pulled solution at time step
t
, and
\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(j)}=\{(S_{s}^{(j)},\{W_{i}(s)^{(k)}\}_{s\in S_{s}^{(k)}})\}^{t}_{s=1}
be the sequence of selected solutions and observed rewards up to and including time step
t
.

For simplicity, we abbreviate

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(k)}}
,
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(k)}}
, as

\mathbb{E}_{k}
,
\mathbb{P}_{k}
respectively.

C.2

Problem-dependent Lower bound

In order to provide a better understanding of the analysis, we first derive a lower bound with Gaussian distributions (unbounded) in Theorem
C.4
. With the same technique, we derive a lower bound with bounded Bernoulli distributions in Theorem
4.3
, which corroborates with our problem setup.

Theorem C.4

(Problem-dependent lower bound for sub-Gaussian instances)
.

Let
\{\delta_{T}\}_{T=1}^{\infty}\in o(1)
be a sequence that satisfies
\ln(1/\delta_{T})=o(T^{b})
for all
b>0
. There exists an instance
\Lambda
, for any
\{\delta_{T}\}_{T\in\mathbb{N}}
-variance-constrained consistent algorithm
\pi
, the regret is lower bounded by

\displaystyle\Omega

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{i\in E}\frac{\ln T}{\Delta_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B},\min}}\right)+\frac{\mu^{\star}}{K}\cdot\Omega\bigg(\frac{K\cdot\ln(1/\delta_{T})}{(\Delta_{S^{\star}})^{2}}+\sum_{i\in E}\Big(\Psi^{\prime}_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}}+\frac{\ln T}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{i,\mathcal{R}})^{2}}+\Phi_{i,\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}}\Big)\bigg).

(385)

Proof.

Given a vanishing sequence
\{\delta_{T}\}_{T\in\mathbb{N}}
, we consider a fixed
\{\delta_{T}\}_{T\in\mathbb{N}}
-variance-constrained consistent algorithm
\pi
on the
K
-path semi-bandit problem.
For simplicity, the distributions of the items are assumed to be Gaussian in the proof, but the techniques can be applied to the Bernoulli case and we provide the instance design and the corresponding bound at the end of the proof.

Under instance
\Lambda_{0}
(the base instance),
\sigma^{2}=\frac{2\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{K}
(so the absolutely safe solutions contain at most
K/2
items) and the distributions of the items are

\displaystyle\nu_{i}^{(0)}=N(\mu_{j}^{(0)},(\sigma_{i}^{2})^{(0)})=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\nu_{P_{1}}^{(0)}=N(\Delta,\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}-\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}}{K}),&&\quad i\in P_{1}\\
&\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)}=N(\Delta-\frac{\epsilon^{\mu}}{K},\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}-\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}}{K}),&&\quad i\in P_{j},2\leq j\leq L_{1}+1\\
&\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)}=N(\Delta+\frac{\epsilon^{\mu}}{K},\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}+\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}}{K}),&&\quad i\in P_{j},L_{1}+2\leq j\leq L_{2}+1\\
&\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)}=N(\Delta-\frac{\epsilon^{\mu}}{K},\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}+\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}}{K}),&&\quad i\in P_{j},L_{2}+2\leq j\leq L_{0}\end{aligned}\right.

(386)

where
\epsilon^{\mu}<\frac{\Delta}{K}
and
\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}
are small positive constants (e.g.
\epsilon^{\mu}=\frac{\Delta}{2K},\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}\leq\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{K^{2}}
), and
L_{1}=L_{2}-L_{1}=L_{0}-L_{2}-1=\frac{L_{0}-1}{3}
(assume it is an integer). In this case,

•

path
1
is an optimal safe path,

•

path
2
to path
L_{1}+1
are the safe and suboptimal paths,

•

path
L_{1}+2
to path
L_{2}+1
are the risky paths

•

path
L_{2}+2
to path
L_{3}+1=L_{0}
are the unsafe and suboptimal paths.

In the following, we will compute the minimum regret yielded from each of the paths.

Case 1: the optimal safe path
P_{1}

In order to achieve
o(T^{a}),\forall a>0
regret, any algorithm has to identify the safeness of the optimal safe solution
P_{1}
and sample
P_{1}

\Omega(T)
times, otherwise, the regret is linear.
According to Lemma
C.3
,
the expected number of time steps needed for the safeness identification of
P_{1}
is lower bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}[M_{1}(\tau)]\geq\sup_{\nu_{P_{1}}^{(1)}\in E(\nu_{P_{1}}^{(0)})}\frac{1}{K-1}\cdot\frac{d(\delta_{T},1-\delta_{T})}{\mathrm{KL}(\nu_{P_{1}}^{(0)},\nu_{P_{1}}^{(1)})}:=T(\nu_{P_{1}}^{(0)})

where

E(\nu_{P_{1}}^{(0)})=\{\nu_{P_{1}}^{(1)}:\text{ the variance associated to }\nu_{P_{1}}^{(1)}\text{ is larger than }\bar{\sigma}^{2}/K\}
.
In particular, we let instance
\Lambda_{1}=(E,\mathcal{A}_{K},\nu^{(1)},\bar{\sigma}^{2})
with

\displaystyle\nu_{i}^{(1)}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\nu_{P_{1}}^{(1)}=N\left(\Delta,\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}+\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}_{1}}{K}\right),&&\quad i\in P_{1}\\
&\nu_{i}^{(0)},&&\quad i\notin P_{1}\end{aligned}\right.

(387)

where
\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}_{1}
is an arbitrarily small constant.
Thus, we can take supremum over
\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}_{1}>0
and have

\displaystyle T(\nu_{P_{1}}^{(0)})\geq\frac{1}{K-1}\cdot\frac{\ln\frac{1}{2.4\delta_{T}}}{\mathrm{KL}(N(\Delta,\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}-\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}}{K}),N(\Delta,\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{K}))}\geq\frac{1}{K-1}\cdot\frac{4K^{2}(\bar{\sigma}^{2}/K)^{2}}{(\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln\frac{1}{2.4\delta_{T}}=\frac{K(\sigma^{2})^{2}}{(\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln\frac{1}{2.4\delta_{T}}

(388)

When a solution
S\subset P_{1}
is chosen before this time step, the instantaneous regret is lower bounded by
\Delta
.
The accumulative regret from
P_{1}
is lower bounded by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}[1](T)\geq\Delta\cdot T(\nu_{P_{1}}^{(0)})=\Omega(\frac{K\Delta}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{P_{1}})^{2}}\ln\frac{1}{\delta_{T}})

(389)

Case 2: the safe and suboptimal paths

For any safe and suboptimal path
P_{j}(2\leq j\leq L_{1}+1)
,
we define instance
\Lambda_{j}=(E,\mathcal{A}_{K},\nu^{(j)},\bar{\sigma}^{2})
with

\displaystyle\nu_{i}^{(j)}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\nu_{P_{j}}^{(j)}=N(\Delta+\frac{\epsilon^{\mu}_{j}}{K},\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}-\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}}{K}),&&\quad i\in P_{j}\\
&\nu_{i}^{(0)},&&\quad i\notin P_{j}\end{aligned}\right.

(390)

where
\epsilon^{\mu}_{j}<\Delta
is an arbitrarily small constant. So
P_{j}
is the optimal safe solution under instance
j
.

Fix any item
i\in P_{j}
, consider the event
\mathcal{E}_{j}=\{N_{i}(T)\geq\frac{T}{2}\}
, under instance
1
,
\mathcal{E}_{j}
indicates the optimal safe solution
P_{1}
is sampled less than
\frac{T}{2}
times; under instance
j
,
\mathcal{E}_{j}^{c}
indicates the optimal safe solution
P_{j}
is sampled less than
\frac{T}{2}
times. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{0}}(T)+\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{j}}(T)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{T}{2}\epsilon^{\mu}\mathbb{P}_{0}[\mathcal{E}_{j}]+\frac{T}{2}\epsilon^{\mu}_{j}\mathbb{P}_{j}[\mathcal{E}_{j}^{c}]

(391)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{T}{2}\min\{\epsilon^{\mu},\epsilon^{\mu}_{j}\}\left(\mathbb{P}_{0}[\mathcal{E}_{j}]+\mathbb{P}_{j}[\mathcal{E}_{j}^{c}]\right)

(392)

According to Lemma
C.1
and Lemma
C.2
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}[\mathcal{E}_{j}]+\mathbb{P}_{j}[\mathcal{E}_{j}^{c}]\geq\frac{1}{2}\exp\{-\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{j})\}

(393)

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{j})=\sum_{i=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}_{0}[N_{i}(T)]\cdot\mathrm{KL}\big(\nu_{i}^{(0)},\nu_{i}^{(j)}\big)=\sum_{i\in P_{j}}\mathbb{E}_{0}[N_{i}(T)]\cdot\mathrm{KL}\big(\nu_{i}^{(0)},\nu_{i}^{(j)}\big)

(394)

Thus

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{0}}(T)+\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{j}}(T)\geq\frac{T}{2}\min\{\epsilon^{\mu},\epsilon^{\mu}_{j}\}\cdot\frac{1}{2}\exp\{-\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{j})\}

(395)

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\frac{\ln\left(\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{0}}(T)+\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{j}}(T)\right)}{\ln T}\geq 1+\frac{\min\{\epsilon^{\mu},\epsilon^{\mu}_{j}\}/4}{\ln T}-\frac{\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{j})}{\ln T}

(396)

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\Longrightarrow}\quad

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{j})}{\ln T}\geq 1

(397)

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i\in P_{j}}\mathbb{E}_{0}[N_{i}(T)]}{\ln T}\geq\frac{1}{\mathrm{KL}\big(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)},\nu_{P_{j}}^{(j)}\big)}=\frac{2\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}-\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}}{K}}{(\frac{\epsilon^{\mu}+\epsilon^{\mu}_{j}}{K})^{2}}

(398)

\displaystyle\overset{(**)}{\Longrightarrow}\quad

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i\in P_{j}}\mathbb{E}_{0}[N_{i}(T)]}{\ln T}\geq\frac{2\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}-\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}}{K}}{(\frac{\epsilon^{\mu}}{K})^{2}}=:T(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)})

(399)

where in
(*)
we let
T\to\infty
and note that both
\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{0}}(T)
and
\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{j}}(T)
are of order
o(T^{a}),\forall a>0
; in
(**)
we take supremum over
\epsilon^{\mu}_{j}>0
.

We also have to take the safeness constraint into consideration.
According to Lemma
C.3
, in order to check the safeness of

P_{j}
, the items in
P_{j}
have to be sampled

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in P_{j}}\mathbb{E}_{\Lambda_{0}}[N_{i}(\tau)]\geq\sup_{\nu_{P_{j}}^{\prime}\in E(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)})}\frac{d(\delta_{T},1-\delta_{T})}{\mathrm{KL}(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)},\nu_{P_{j}}^{\prime})}.

(400)

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i\in P_{j}}\mathbb{E}_{\Lambda_{0}}[N_{i}(\tau)]}{\ln T}\geq\sup_{\nu_{P_{j}}^{\prime}\in E(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)})}\frac{1}{\mathrm{KL}(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)},\nu_{P_{j}}^{\prime})}\cdot\frac{d(\delta_{T},1-\delta_{T})}{\ln T}\geq\frac{4K^{2}(\bar{\sigma}^{2}/K)^{2}}{(\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\frac{\ln\frac{1}{2.4\delta_{T}}}{\ln T}:=T_{\mathrm{safe}}(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)}).

(401)

If
T(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)})\leq T_{\mathrm{safe}}(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)})
, it indicates the suboptimality of
P_{j}
is identified before the safeness. Furthermore, whenever a solution
S\subset P_{j}
is sampled,
S
can have most
K-1
items. So the regret is lower bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{Reg}[j](T)}{\ln T}\geq\frac{T(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)})}{K-1}\cdot(\epsilon^{\mu}+\Delta-\frac{\epsilon^{\mu}}{K})\geq\frac{2K^{2}\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}-\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}}{K}}{(\epsilon^{\mu})^{2}}\cdot(\frac{\Delta}{K-1}+\frac{\epsilon^{\mu}}{K})

(402)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{Reg}[j](T)}{\ln T}\geq\frac{2K\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}-\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}}{K}}{(\epsilon^{\mu})^{2}}\cdot(\Delta+\epsilon^{\mu})\geq\frac{K\sigma^{2}/2}{(\epsilon^{\mu})^{2}}\cdot(\Delta+\epsilon^{\mu})

(403)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}[j](T)=\Omega\left(\frac{K}{\Delta_{P_{j}}^{2}}(\Delta_{P_{j}}+\Delta)\ln T\right)

(404)

If
T(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)})\geq T_{\mathrm{safe}}(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)})
, it indicates the suboptimality of
P_{j}
is identified after the safeness. Thus, whenever a solution
S\subset P_{j}
is sampled,
S
can have most
K-1
items before the safeness checking is finished. So the regret is lower bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{Reg}[j](T)}{\ln T}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{T_{\mathrm{safe}}(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)})}{K-1}\cdot(\epsilon^{\mu}+\Delta-\frac{\epsilon^{\mu}}{K})+\frac{T(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)})-T_{\mathrm{safe}}(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)})}{K}\cdot\epsilon^{\mu}

(405)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{T(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)})}{K}\cdot\epsilon^{\mu}+(\Delta-\frac{\epsilon^{\mu}}{K})\cdot\frac{T_{\mathrm{safe}}(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)})}{K-1}

(406)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2K\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}-\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}}{K}}{(\epsilon^{\mu})^{2}}\cdot\epsilon^{\mu}+(\Delta-\frac{\epsilon^{\mu}}{K})\cdot\frac{1}{K-1}\cdot\frac{4K^{2}(\bar{\sigma}^{2}/K)^{2}}{(\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\frac{\ln\frac{1}{2.4\delta_{T}}}{\ln T}

(407)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{K\sigma^{2}/2}{\epsilon^{\mu}}+(\Delta-\frac{\epsilon^{\mu}}{K})\cdot\frac{1}{K-1}\cdot\frac{K^{2}(\sigma^{2})^{2}}{(\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\frac{\ln\frac{1}{2.4\delta_{T}}}{\ln T}

(408)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}[j](T)=\Omega\left(\frac{K}{\Delta_{P_{j}}}\ln T+(\Delta-\frac{\Delta_{P_{j}}}{K})\frac{K}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{P_{j}})^{2}}\ln\frac{1}{\delta_{T}}\right)

(409)

Based on (
404
) and (
409
), the regret is

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}[j](T)=\Omega\left(\frac{K}{\Delta_{P_{j}}}\ln T+\Delta\cdot\min\left\{\frac{K}{\Delta_{P_{j}}^{2}}\ln T,\frac{K}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{P_{j}})^{2}}\ln\frac{1}{\delta_{T}}\right\}\right)

(410)

Case 3: the risky paths

For any risky path
P_{j}
(
L_{1}+2\leq j\leq L_{2}+1
, we define instance
\Lambda_{j}=(E,\mathcal{A}_{K},\nu^{(j)},\bar{\sigma}^{2})
with

\displaystyle\nu_{i}^{(j)}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\nu_{P_{j}}^{(j)}=N(\Delta+\frac{\epsilon^{\mu}}{K},\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}-\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}_{j}}{K}),&&\quad i\in P_{j}\\
&\nu_{i}^{(0)},&&\quad i\notin P_{j}\end{aligned}\right.

(411)

where
\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}_{j}
is an arbitrarily small constant. So
P_{j}
is the optimal safe solution under instance
j
.

Fix any item
i\in P_{j}
, consider the event
\mathcal{E}_{j}=\{N_{i}(T)\geq\frac{T}{2}\}
, under instance
1
,
\mathcal{E}_{j}
indicates the optimal safe solution
P_{1}
is sampled less than
\frac{T}{2}
times; under instance
j
,
\mathcal{E}_{j}^{c}
indicates the optimal safe solution
P_{j}
is sampled less than
\frac{T}{2}
times. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{0}}(T)+\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{j}}(T)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{T}{2}\left(\Delta-\frac{K-1}{K}\epsilon^{\mu}\right)\mathbb{P}_{0}[\mathcal{E}_{j}]+\frac{T}{2}\epsilon^{\mu}\mathbb{P}_{j}[\mathcal{E}_{j}^{c}]

(412)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{T}{2}\epsilon^{\mu}\cdot\left(\mathbb{P}_{0}[\mathcal{E}_{j}]+\mathbb{P}_{j}[\mathcal{E}_{j}^{c}]\right)

(413)

According to Lemma
C.1
and Lemma
C.2
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}[\mathcal{E}_{j}]+\mathbb{P}_{j}[\mathcal{E}_{j}^{c}]\geq\frac{1}{2}\exp\{-\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{j})\}

(414)

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{j})=\sum_{i=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}_{0}[N_{i}(T)]\cdot\mathrm{KL}\big(\nu_{i}^{(0)},\nu_{i}^{(j)}\big)=\sum_{i\in P_{j}}\mathbb{E}_{0}[N_{i}(T)]\cdot\mathrm{KL}\big(\nu_{i}^{(0)},\nu_{i}^{(j)}\big)

(415)

Thus

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{0}}(T)+\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{j}}(T)\geq\frac{T}{2}\epsilon^{\mu}\cdot\frac{1}{2}\exp\{-\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{j})\}

(416)

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\frac{\ln\left(\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{0}}(T)+\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{j}}(T)\right)}{\ln T}\geq 1+\frac{\min\{\epsilon^{\mu},\epsilon^{\mu}_{j}\}/4}{\ln T}-\frac{\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{j})}{\ln T}

(417)

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\Longrightarrow}\quad

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{j})}{\ln T}\geq 1

(418)

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i\in P_{j}}\mathbb{E}_{0}[N_{i}(T)]}{\ln T}\geq\frac{1}{\mathrm{KL}\big(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)},\nu_{P_{j}}^{(j)}\big)}=\frac{K^{2}(\sigma^{2})^{2}}{(\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}=:T(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)})

(419)

where in
(*)
we let
T\to\infty
and note that both
\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{0}}(T)
and
\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{j}}(T)
are of order
o(T^{a}),\forall a>0
.

Note that
P_{j}
is a risky path and if any solution
S\subset P_{j}
is sampled,
|S|\leq K-1
. Thus,
\mathbb{E}[M_{j}(T)]\geq\frac{T(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)})}{K-1}\ln T
. The regret is lower bounded by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}[j](T)

\displaystyle=\Omega\left(\left(\Delta-\frac{K-1}{K}\epsilon^{\mu}\right)\frac{1}{K-1}\frac{K^{2}(\sigma^{2})^{2}}{(\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln T\right)

(420)

\displaystyle=\Omega\left(\frac{K\Delta}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{P_{j}})^{2}}\ln T\right)

(421)

Case 4: the unsafe and suboptimal paths

For any unsafe and suboptimal path
P_{j}(L_{2}+2\leq j\leq L_{0})
,
we define instance
\Lambda_{j}=(E,\mathcal{A}_{K},\nu^{(j)},\bar{\sigma}^{2})
with

\displaystyle\nu_{i}^{(j)}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\nu_{P_{j}}^{(j)}=N(\Delta+\frac{\epsilon^{\mu}_{j}}{K},\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}-\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}_{j}}{K}),&&\quad i\in P_{j}\\
&\nu_{i}^{(0)},&&\quad i\notin P_{j}\end{aligned}\right.

(422)

where
\epsilon^{\mu}_{j}<\Delta,\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}_{j}<\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{K}
are arbitrarily small constants. So
P_{j}
is the optimal safe solution under instance
j
.

Fix any item
i\in P_{j}
, consider the event
\mathcal{E}_{j}=\{N_{i}(T)\geq\frac{T}{2}\}
, under instance
1
,
\mathcal{E}_{j}
indicates the optimal safe solution
P_{1}
is sampled less than
\frac{T}{2}
times; under instance
j
,
\mathcal{E}_{j}^{c}
indicates the optimal safe solution
P_{j}
is sampled less than
\frac{T}{2}
times. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{0}}(T)+\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{j}}(T)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{T}{2}\epsilon^{\mu}\mathbb{P}_{0}[\mathcal{E}_{j}]+\frac{T}{2}\epsilon^{\mu}_{j}\mathbb{P}_{j}[\mathcal{E}_{j}^{c}]

(423)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{T}{2}\min\{\epsilon^{\mu},\epsilon^{\mu}_{j}\}\left(\mathbb{P}_{0}[\mathcal{E}_{j}]+\mathbb{P}_{j}[\mathcal{E}_{j}^{c}]\right)

(424)

According to Lemma
C.1
and Lemma
C.2
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}[\mathcal{E}_{j}]+\mathbb{P}_{j}[\mathcal{E}_{j}^{c}]\geq\frac{1}{2}\exp\{-\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{j})\}

(425)

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{j})=\sum_{i=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}_{0}[N_{i}(T)]\cdot\mathrm{KL}\big(\nu_{i}^{(0)},\nu_{i}^{(j)}\big)=\sum_{i\in P_{j}}\mathbb{E}_{0}[N_{i}(T)]\cdot\mathrm{KL}\big(\nu_{i}^{(0)},\nu_{i}^{(j)}\big)

(426)

Thus

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{0}}(T)+\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{j}}(T)\geq\frac{T}{2}\min\{\epsilon^{\mu},\epsilon^{\mu}_{j}\}\cdot\frac{1}{2}\exp\{-\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{j})\}

(427)

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\frac{\ln\left(\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{0}}(T)+\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{j}}(T)\right)}{\ln T}\geq 1+\frac{\min\{\epsilon^{\mu},\epsilon^{\mu}_{j}\}/4}{\ln T}-\frac{\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{j})}{\ln T}

(428)

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\Longrightarrow}\quad

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{j})}{\ln T}\geq 1

(429)

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i\in P_{j}}\mathbb{E}_{0}[N_{i}(T)]}{\ln T}\geq\frac{1}{\mathrm{KL}\big(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)},\nu_{P_{j}}^{(j)}\big)}=4K^{2}\left(\left(\frac{\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}_{j}+\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}}{(\bar{\sigma}^{2}-\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}_{j})/K}\right)^{2}+\frac{2(\epsilon^{\mu}_{j}+\epsilon^{\mu})^{2}}{(\bar{\sigma}^{2}-\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}_{j})/K}\right)^{-1}

(430)

\displaystyle\overset{(**)}{\Longrightarrow}\quad

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i\in P_{j}}\mathbb{E}_{0}[N_{i}(T)]}{\ln T}\geq 4K^{2}\left(\left(\frac{\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}}{\sigma^{2}/2}\right)^{2}+\frac{2(\epsilon^{\mu})^{2}}{\sigma^{2}/2}\right)^{-1}=:T(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)})

(431)

where in
(*)
we let
T\to\infty
and note that both
\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{0}}(T)
and
\mathrm{Reg}_{\Lambda_{j}}(T)
are of order
o(T^{a}),\forall a>0
; in
(**)
we take the supremum over
\epsilon^{\mu}_{j}>0,\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}_{j}>0
.

Note that
P_{j}
is an unsafe path and if any solution
S\subset P_{j}
is sampled,
|S|\leq K-1
. Thus,
\mathbb{E}[M_{j}(T)]\geq\frac{T(\nu_{P_{j}}^{(0)})}{K-1}\ln T
. The regret is lower bounded by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}[j](T)

\displaystyle\geq\left(\Delta+\frac{K-1}{K}\epsilon^{\mu}\right)\frac{4K^{2}}{K-1}\left(\left(\frac{\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}}{\sigma^{2}/2}\right)^{2}+\frac{2(\epsilon^{\mu})^{2}}{\sigma^{2}/2}\right)^{-1}\ln T

(432)

\displaystyle=\Omega\left(\frac{K\Delta+K\epsilon^{\mu}}{\max\{\epsilon^{\mu},\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}\}^{2}}\ln T\right)

(433)

\displaystyle=\Omega\left(\min\left\{\frac{K\Delta}{\Delta_{P_{j}}^{2}}\ln T,\frac{K\Delta}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{P_{j}})^{2}}\ln T\right\}\right)

(434)

In conclusion
,
the regret yielded from these paths is lower bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{Reg}(T)}{1-\delta_{T}}

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{Reg}[1](T)+\sum_{j=2}^{L_{1}+1}\mathrm{Reg}[j](T)+\sum_{j=L_{1}+2}^{L_{2}+1}\mathrm{Reg}[j](T)+\sum_{j=L_{2}+2}^{L_{0}}\mathrm{Reg}[j](T)

(435)

\displaystyle\geq\Omega\left(\frac{K\Delta}{(\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln\frac{1}{\delta_{T}}\right)+\sum_{j=2}^{L_{1}+1}\Omega\left(\frac{K}{\epsilon^{\mu}}\ln T+\Delta\cdot\min\left\{\frac{K}{(\epsilon^{\mu})^{2}}\ln T,\frac{K}{(\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln\frac{1}{\delta_{T}}\right\}\right)

(436)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=L_{1}+2}^{L_{2}+1}\Omega\left(\frac{K\Delta}{(\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln T\right)+\sum_{j=L_{2}+2}^{L_{0}}\Omega\left(\min\left\{\frac{K\Delta}{(\epsilon^{\mu})^{2}}\ln T,\frac{K\Delta}{(\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln T\right\}\right).

(437)

Note

•

L_{1}=L_{2}-L_{1}=L_{0}-L_{2}-1=\frac{L_{0}-1}{3}
,
L_{0}=\frac{L}{K}
and
\mu^{\star}=K\Delta
.

•

for
S^{\star}
,
\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}=\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{S^{\star}}
.

•

for
S\in\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}
and
i\in S
, we check that if
S\subset P_{j},2\leq j\leq L_{1}+1
,
\Delta_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B},\min}=\epsilon^{\mu}
and

\displaystyle\Psi^{\prime}_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}}\geq\min\left\{\frac{\ln T}{\Delta_{S}^{2}},\frac{9\ln(1/\delta_{T})}{(\Delta_{S}^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\right\}.

(438)

where the equality holds when
S=P_{j},2\leq j\leq L_{1}+1
.

•

for
S\in\mathcal{R}
and
i\in S
,
\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}=\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{i,\mathcal{R}}
.

•

S\in\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}
and
i\in S
, we can easily check
S=P_{j}
for some
L_{2}+2\leq j\leq L_{0}
, thus
\Delta_{i,\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B},\min}=\epsilon^{\mu}
and

\displaystyle\Phi_{i,\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}}=\min\left\{\frac{\ln T}{\Delta_{S}^{2}},\frac{9\ln T}{(\Delta_{S}^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\right\}.

(439)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}(T)

\displaystyle\geq\Omega\left(\frac{L\ln T}{\Delta_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B},\min}}\right)+\frac{\mu^{\star}}{K}\cdot\Omega\left(\frac{K\cdot\ln(1/\delta_{T})}{(\Delta_{S^{\star}})^{2}}+\sum_{i\in E}\Psi^{\prime}_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}}+\sum_{i\in E}\frac{\ln T}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{i,\mathcal{R}})^{2}}+\sum_{i\in E}\Phi_{i,\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}}\right).

(440)

∎

See
4.3

Proof.

We divide the whole ground set into several paths.
Meanwhile, we define four sets
E_{1},\ldots,E_{4}
such that
E_{i}\cap E_{j}=\emptyset
for any
i\neq j
.
P_{1}=E_{1}
,
P_{j}\subset E_{j}
for
j=2,3,4
. For any
j>4
, there exists
k\neq 1
such that
P_{j}\subset E_{k}
.
For any path
P_{j}
, if
P_{j}\subset E_{4}
,
|P_{j}|=K_{2}
; otherwise,
|P_{j}|=K_{1}
.
P_{j}
consists of arms

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}|P_{i}|+1,\ldots,\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}|P_{i}|+K_{1}\cdot\mathbbm{1}\{P_{j}\not\subset E_{4}\}+K_{2}\cdot\mathbbm{1}\{P_{j}\subset E_{4}\}.

The feasible solution set
\mathcal{A}_{K}
consists of all subsets of each path.

We let
\mathrm{Bern}(a)
denote the Bernoulli distribution with parameter
a(a\in(0,1))
. Note that the variance of
\mathrm{Bern}(a)
is
a(1-a)
.
We construct an instance with
\nu_{i}=\mathrm{Bern}(\mu_{i})
.
With
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}>0
, we set

\displaystyle\mu_{i}=\Delta\quad\forall i\in E_{1},

\displaystyle\mu_{i}=\Delta-\varepsilon_{1}\quad\forall i\in E_{2},

\displaystyle\mu_{i}=\Delta+\varepsilon_{2}\quad\forall i\in E_{3},

\displaystyle\mu_{i}=\Delta-\varepsilon_{3}\quad\forall i\in E_{4}.

We let
2\leq K_{1}\ <K_{2}\leq K
,
\Delta<1/2
and

\displaystyle K_{1}\Delta(1-\Delta)<\bar{\sigma}^{2}\text{ (paths in $E_{1}$ and $E_{2}$ are safe)},

\displaystyle K_{1}(\Delta+\varepsilon_{2})(1-\Delta-\varepsilon_{2})>\bar{\sigma}^{2}\text{ (paths in $E_{3}$ are unsafe)},

\displaystyle K_{2}(\Delta-\varepsilon_{3})(1-\Delta+\varepsilon_{3})>\bar{\sigma}\text{ (paths in $E_{4}$ are unsafe)},

\displaystyle K_{2}(\Delta-\varepsilon_{3})<K_{1}\Delta\text{ (paths in $E_{4}$ are suboptimal)},

\displaystyle(K_{1}-1)\cdot(\Delta+\varepsilon_{2})<K_{1}\cdot\Delta~\Leftrightarrow~(K_{1}-1)\cdot\varepsilon_{2}<\Delta~\Leftrightarrow~\varepsilon_{2}<\frac{\Delta}{K_{1}-1}\text{ (paths in $E_{3}$ are suboptimal or unsafe)}.

The conditions above indicate that

•

P_{1}
is the unique optimal safe set;

•

if
P_{j}\subset E_{2}
,
P_{j}
and its subsets are safe but suboptimal;

•

if
P_{j}\subset E_{3}
,
P_{j}
is risky, and its proper subsets are suboptimal;

•

if
P_{j}\subset E_{4}
,
P_{j}
and its subsets are suboptimal, and
P_{j}
is unsafe.

Let

\displaystyle p_{1}:=\frac{1-\sqrt{1-\bar{\sigma}^{2}/K_{1}}}{2}~\text{ and }~p_{2}:=\frac{1-\sqrt{1-\bar{\sigma}^{2}/K_{2}}}{2},

The relations between
\mu_{i}
’s are as in the following figure:

With a similar proof to that of Theorem
C.4
, we have,
the accumulative regret from the optimal
P_{1}
is lower bounded by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}[1](T)\geq\Omega(\frac{K_{1}\Delta}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{P_{1}})^{2}}\ln\frac{1}{\delta_{T}});

(441)

the accumulative regret from a safe and suboptimal path in
E_{2}
is lower bounded by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}[j](T)\geq\Omega\left(\frac{K_{1}}{\Delta_{P_{j}}}\ln T+\Delta\cdot\min\left\{\frac{K_{1}}{\Delta_{P_{j}}^{2}}\ln T,\frac{K_{1}}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{P_{j}})^{2}}\ln\frac{1}{\delta_{T}}\right\}\right);

(442)

the accumulative regret from a risky path in
E_{3}
is lower bounded by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}[j](T)

\displaystyle\geq\Omega\left(\frac{K_{1}\Delta}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{P_{j}})^{2}}\ln T\right);

(443)

the accumulative regret from a unsafe and suboptimal path in
E_{4}
is lower bounded by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}[j](T)

\displaystyle\geq\Omega\left(\min\left\{\frac{K_{2}\Delta}{\Delta_{P_{j}}^{2}}\ln T,\frac{K_{2}\Delta}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{P_{j}})^{2}}\ln T\right\}\right).

(444)

We let

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mu}=\min_{j}\{\Delta_{P_{j}}\},\quad\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}=\min_{j}\{\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{P_{j}}\};

and set
K_{1}
,
K_{2}
,
\varepsilon_{1}
,
\varepsilon_{2}
,
\varepsilon_{3}
such that

\displaystyle K_{1}>\frac{3}{4}\cdot K_{2},\quad K_{2}=K,\quad\min_{j}\{\Delta_{P_{j}}\}<2\epsilon^{\mu},\quad\min_{j}\{\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{P_{j}}\}<2\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}.

In conclusion
,
the regret yielded from these paths is lower bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{Reg}(T)}{1-\delta_{T}}

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{Reg}[1](T)+\sum_{P_{j}\in E_{2}}\mathrm{Reg}[j](T)+\sum_{P_{j}\in E_{3}}\mathrm{Reg}[j](T)+\sum_{P_{j}\in E_{4}}\mathrm{Reg}[j](T)

(445)

\displaystyle\geq\Omega\left(\frac{K\Delta}{(\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln\frac{1}{\delta_{T}}\right)+|E_{2}|\cdot\Omega\left(\frac{K}{\epsilon^{\mu}}\ln T+\Delta\cdot\min\left\{\frac{K}{(\epsilon^{\mu})^{2}}\ln T,\frac{K}{(\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln\frac{1}{\delta_{T}}\right\}\right)

(446)

\displaystyle\quad+|E_{3}|\cdot\Omega\left(\frac{K\Delta}{(\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln T\right)+|E_{4}|\cdot\Omega\left(\min\left\{\frac{K\Delta}{(\epsilon^{\mu})^{2}}\ln T,\frac{K\Delta}{(\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln T\right\}\right).

(447)

Note that

•

\mu^{\star}=K_{1}\Delta
.

•

for
S^{\star}
,
\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}=\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{S^{\star}}
.

•

for
S\in\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}
and
i\in S
, we check that if
S\subset P_{j},j\in E_{2}
,
\Delta_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B},\min}=\epsilon^{\mu}
and

\displaystyle\Psi^{\prime}_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}}\geq\min\left\{\frac{\ln T}{\Delta_{S}^{2}},\frac{9\ln(1/\delta_{T})}{(\Delta_{S}^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\right\}.

(448)

where the equality holds when
S=P_{j},P_{j}\in E_{2}
.

•

for
S\in\mathcal{R}
and
i\in S
,
\epsilon^{\mathrm{v}}=\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{i,\mathcal{R}}
.

•

S\in\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}
and
i\in S
, we can easily check
S=P_{j}
for some
P_{j}\in E_{4}
, thus
\Delta_{i,\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B},\min}=\epsilon^{\mu}
and

\displaystyle\Phi_{i,\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}}=\min\left\{\frac{\ln T}{\Delta_{S}^{2}},\frac{9\ln T}{(\Delta_{S}^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\right\}.

(449)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}(T)

\displaystyle\geq\Omega\left(\frac{L\ln T}{\Delta_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B},\min}}\right)+\frac{\mu^{\star}}{K}\cdot\Omega\left(\frac{K\cdot\ln(1/\delta_{T})}{(\Delta_{S^{\star}})^{2}}+\sum_{i\in E}\Psi^{\prime}_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}}+\sum_{i\in E}\frac{\ln T}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{i,\mathcal{R}})^{2}}+\sum_{i\in E}\Phi_{i,\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}}\right).

(450)

∎

C.3

Problem-independent Lower bound

See
5.2

Proof.

Since the rewards of items are bounded in
[0,1]
, the variance of each arm is at most
{1}/{4}
.
Therefore, when
\bar{\sigma}^{2}\in[K/4,\infty)
, any solution in
\mathcal{A}_{K}
is safe and there exists a generic lower bound
(
Kveton et al. 2015
)
.
When
\bar{\sigma}^{2}\in(0,K/4)
, let

\displaystyle\bar{a}:=\frac{1+\sqrt{1-\bar{\sigma}^{2}/K}}{2}~\text{ and }~\underline{a}:=\frac{1-\sqrt{1-\bar{\sigma}^{2}/K}}{2}.

We consider the instances containing items with Bernoulli reward distributions. We let
\mathrm{Bern}(a)
denote the Bernoulli distribution with mean
a
. An item
i\in[L]
with reward distribution
\mathrm{Bern}(\mu_{i})
is with variance
\mu_{i}(1-\mu_{i})
, which is smaller than
\bar{\sigma}^{2}/K
if and only if
\mu_{i}\in(0,\underline{a})\cup(\bar{a},1)
.

We will construct
3
instances such that
under instance
k
(
0\leq k\leq 2
), the stochastic reward of an item in path
j
(
1\leq j\leq L_{0}
) is drawn from distribution

\nu_{j}^{(k)}=\mathrm{Bern}(\mu_{j}^{(k)})
,
where
\mu_{j}^{(k)}
will be specified in each case.

Under instance
0
, with
\mu_{0}<\underline{a}
, we let
\mu_{j}^{(0)}=\mu_{0}
for all
j
, i.e., the reward distribution of each item is
\mathrm{Bern}(\mu_{0})
. Since
\mu_{P_{j}}=K\mu_{0}
and
\sigma^{2}_{P_{j}}=K\mu_{0}(1-\mu_{0})<\bar{\sigma}^{2}
for all
j
, each path is an identical safe and optimal solution.
Since all paths are equivalent under instance
0
, we have
\mathbb{E}_{0}[M_{j}(t)]=T/L_{0}
for all
j\in 1,\ldots,L_{0}
, where
\mathbb{E}_{0}
denote the expectation under instance
0
.

We next construct instance
1
such that

\displaystyle\mu_{1}^{(1)}=\mu_{1},\qquad\mu_{j}^{(1)}=\mu_{0}\quad j\neq 1,

where
\mu_{0}<\mu_{1}<\underline{a}
.
2
2

2

In this proof,
\mu_{1}
are
\mu_{2}
are redefined to minimize clutter; the previous definitions of them not used.

Hence,
P_{1}
is the unique optimal safe solution under instance
1
while all other solutions are safe but suboptimal.

With an analysis similar to that of Lemma 6.4 in
Zhong et al. 2021
, we can show that

Lemma C.5
.

Let the reward distribution of item
i
be
\nu_{i}^{(j)}
under instance
j(j=1,2)
, then

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(1)},\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(2)}\big)=\sum_{i=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}_{0}[N_{i}(T)]\cdot\mathrm{KL}\big(\nu_{i}^{(1)},\nu_{i}^{(2)}\big).

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(0)},\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(1)}\big)=\sum_{i\in P_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{0}[N_{i}(T)]\cdot\big(\nu_{i}^{(0)},\nu_{i}^{(1)}\big)\overset{(a)}{\leq}K\cdot\mathbb{E}_{0}[M_{1}(t)]\cdot d(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})=K\cdot\frac{T}{L_{0}}\cdot d(\mu_{0},\mu_{1}),

where
(a)
follows from the fact that at most
K
items are selected at one time step and the definition of instances.

Next, we apply Pinsker’s Inequality to bound
\mathbb{E}_{1}[M_{1}(T)]
.
Lemma
C.1
indicates that

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}_{0}[M_{1}(T)]-\mathbb{E}_{1}[M_{1}(T)]\right|\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{KL}\big(\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(0)},\mathcal{H}_{T}^{(1)}\big)},

\displaystyle\Rightarrow~

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}_{1}[M_{1}(T)]-\frac{T}{L_{0}}\right|\leq\sqrt{\frac{KT}{2L_{0}}\cdot d(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})}.

Moreover, since the paths
P_{j}
for
j\neq 1
are identical under instance
1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{1}[M_{j}(T)]=\frac{1}{L_{0}-1}\big(T-\mathbb{E}_{1}[M_{1}(T)]\big)\geq\frac{1}{L_{0}-1}\Bigg(T-\frac{T}{L_{0}}-\sqrt{\frac{KT}{2L_{0}}\cdot d(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})}~\Bigg):=\underline{M}.

To learn the regret incurred by
P_{2}
under instance
1
, we need to take the effects of the safety constraint into consideration.
Lemma
C.3
indicates that

•

if
\underline{M}<T(\nu_{0})
, at each of the first
\underline{M}
time steps in
S_{2}(T)
, at most
K-1
items are pulled and regret of at least
[K(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})+\mu_{2}]\cdot\underline{M}
is incurred, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}[2]\geq[K(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})+\mu_{2}]\cdot\underline{M}.

•

if
\underline{M}\geq T(\nu_{0})
, at each of the first
T(\nu_{0})
time steps in
S_{2}(T)
, at most
K-1
items are pulled and regret of at least
[K(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})+\mu_{2}]\cdot T(\nu_{0})
is incurred; besides, at the subsequent time steps in
S_{2}(T)
, regret of at least
K(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\cdot[\underline{M}-T(\nu_{0})]
is incurred, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}[2]\geq[K(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})+\mu_{2}]\cdot T(\nu_{0})+K(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\cdot[\underline{M}-T(\nu_{0})]=K(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\cdot\underline{M}+\mu_{2}\cdot T(\nu_{0}).

In short, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}[2]\geq K(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\cdot\underline{M}+\mu_{2}\cdot\min\{T(\nu_{0}),\underline{M}\}.

We can lower bound
\mathrm{Reg}(j)
for all
j=3,\ldots,L_{0}
with the same method.

Besides, since

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{1}[M_{1}(T)]\geq T-\sqrt{\frac{KT}{2L_{0}}\cdot d(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})}

and at most
K-1
items are selected at each of the first
T(\nu_{1})
time steps in
S_{1}(T)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}[1]\geq\mu_{1}\cdot\min\bigg\{T(\nu_{1}),T-\sqrt{\frac{KT}{2L_{0}}\cdot d(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})}\bigg\}.

Therefore, under instance
1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{Reg}(T)}{1-\delta}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{j=1}^{L_{0}}\mathrm{Reg}[j]

\displaystyle\geq\mu_{1}\cdot\min\bigg\{T(\nu_{1}),T-\sqrt{\frac{KT}{2L_{0}}\cdot d(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})}\bigg\}+(L_{0}-1)\cdot[K(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\cdot\underline{M}+\mu_{2}\cdot\min\{T(\nu_{0}),\underline{M}\}]

\displaystyle=\mu_{1}\cdot\min\bigg\{\sup_{\nu_{1}^{\prime}\in E(\nu_{1})}\frac{1}{K-1}\cdot\frac{d(\delta,1-\delta)}{\mathrm{KL}(\nu_{1},\nu_{1}^{\prime})},\,T-\sqrt{\frac{KT}{2L_{0}}\cdot d(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+(L_{0}-1)\cdot\bigg[K(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\cdot\underline{M}+\mu_{2}\cdot\min\bigg\{\sup_{\nu_{0}^{\prime}\in E(\nu_{0})}\frac{1}{K-1}\cdot\frac{d(\delta,1-\delta)}{\mathrm{KL}(\nu_{0},\nu_{0}^{\prime})},\,\underline{M}\bigg\}\bigg]

where

\displaystyle E(\nu_{0})=\{\nu(0^{\prime}):\text{ the variance related to }\nu(0^{\prime})\text{ is larger than }\bar{\sigma}^{2}/K\},

\displaystyle E(\nu_{1})=\{\nu(1^{\prime}):\text{ the variance related to }\nu(1^{\prime})\text{ is larger than }\bar{\sigma}^{2}/K\},

\displaystyle\underline{M}=\frac{1}{L_{0}-1}\Bigg(T-\frac{T}{L_{0}}-\sqrt{\frac{KT}{2L_{0}}\cdot d(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})}~\Bigg).

By Pinsker’s inequality (see Lemma
C.1
),
for
\mu_{i}\geq 1/2
,

\displaystyle d(\mu_{i},\bar{a})\leq\frac{(\mu_{i}-\bar{a})^{2}\cdot(1-\mu_{i}-\bar{a})^{2}}{\underline{a}(1-\mu_{i}-\bar{a})^{2}}=\frac{[\mu_{i}(1-\mu_{i})-\bar{\sigma}^{2}/K]^{2}}{\underline{a}(1-\mu_{i}-\bar{a})^{2}}\leq\frac{[\mu_{i}(1-\mu_{i})-\bar{\sigma}^{2}/K]^{2}}{\underline{a}(\bar{a}-1/2)^{2}}=\frac{[\mu_{i}(1-\mu_{i})-\bar{\sigma}^{2}/K]^{2}}{\underline{a}(1/2-\underline{a})^{2}};

for
\mu_{i}<1/2
,

\displaystyle d(\mu_{i},\underline{a})\leq\frac{(\mu_{i}-\underline{a})^{2}\cdot(1-\mu_{i}-\underline{a})^{2}}{\underline{a}(1-\mu_{i}-\underline{a})^{2}}=\frac{[\mu_{i}(1-\mu_{i})-\bar{\sigma}^{2}/K]^{2}}{\underline{a}(1-\mu_{i}-\underline{a})^{2}}\leq\frac{[\mu_{i}(1-\mu_{i})-\bar{\sigma}^{2}/K]^{2}}{\underline{a}(1/2-\underline{a})^{2}}.

Since
\nu_{0}=\mathrm{Bern}(\mu_{0})
,
\nu_{1}=\mathrm{Bern}(\mu_{1})
, and
0<\mu_{0}<\mu_{1}<\underline{a}<1/2
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\nu_{0}^{\prime}\in E(\nu_{0})}\frac{1}{K-1}\cdot\frac{d(\delta,1-\delta)}{\mathrm{KL}(\nu_{0},\nu_{0}^{\prime})}=\frac{d(\delta,1-\delta)}{K-1}\cdot\frac{(1/2-\underline{a})^{2}}{[\mu_{0}(0-\mu_{0})-\bar{\sigma}^{2}/K]^{2}},

\displaystyle\sup_{\nu_{1}^{\prime}\in E(\nu_{1})}\frac{1}{K-1}\cdot\frac{d(\delta,1-\delta)}{\mathrm{KL}(\nu_{1},\nu_{1}^{\prime})}=\frac{d(\delta,1-\delta)}{K-1}\cdot\frac{(1/2-\underline{a})^{2}}{[\mu_{1}(1-\mu_{1})-\bar{\sigma}^{2}/K]^{2}}.

We define the minimum variance gap
\Delta^{v}:=\min_{S\in\mathcal{A}}\Delta_{S}^{v}
.
When
K^{3}\leq L^{2}
and
LK/T\leq\underline{a}^{2}
, we can let
\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}=\sqrt{L/KT}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}(T)

\displaystyle=\Omega\left(\min\left\{\frac{1}{K-1}\cdot\frac{d(\delta,1-\delta)}{(\Delta^{v}/K)^{2}},T\right\}+\sqrt{KLT}+L_{0}\cdot\min\left\{\frac{1}{K-1}\cdot\frac{d(\delta,1-\delta)}{(\Delta^{v}/K)^{2}},\frac{TK}{L}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\Omega\left(\min\left\{K\cdot\frac{d(\delta,1-\delta)}{(\Delta^{v}/)^{2}},T\right\}+\sqrt{KLT}+L\cdot\min\left\{\frac{d(\delta,1-\delta)}{(\Delta^{v})^{2}},\frac{T}{L}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\Omega\left(\sqrt{KLT}+\min\left\{L\cdot\frac{d(\delta,1-\delta)}{(\Delta^{v})^{2}},T\right\}\right).

Moreover, we complete the proof with
d(\delta,1-\delta)\geq-\ln(2.4\delta)
and
\delta_{T}=\delta
.

∎

Appendix D

Tightness of the Upper bound

Corollary D.1

(Tightness of problem-dependent bounds)
.

Let
\{\delta_{T}\}_{T=1}^{\infty}\in o(1)
be a sequence that satisfies
\ln(1/\delta_{T})=o(T^{b})
for all
b>0
,

•

if
\ln(1/\delta_{T})\in o(\ln T)
, in particular, if
\delta_{T}=\delta_{0}>0
for all
T
, the regret
\mathrm{Reg}(T)
is

\displaystyle\Omega\left(\sum_{i\in E}\frac{\ln T}{\Delta_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B},\min}}+\frac{\mu^{\star}\ln T/K}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{i,\mathcal{R}})^{2}}+\frac{\mu^{\star}}{K}\Phi_{i,\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}}\right)

(451)

\displaystyle\cap\,

\displaystyle O\left(\sum_{i\in E}\frac{K\ln T}{\Delta_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B},\min}}+\frac{\mu^{\star}K\ln T}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{i,\mathcal{R}})^{2}}+\mu^{\star}K\Phi_{i,\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}}\right)

(452)

•

if
\ln(1/\delta_{T})=\lambda\ln T
, i.e.,
\delta_{T}=T^{-\lambda}
with a fixed
\lambda>0
, the regret
\mathrm{Reg}(T)
is

\displaystyle\Omega\left(\sum_{i\in E}\frac{\ln T}{\Delta_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B},\min}}+\frac{\lambda\mu^{\star}\ln T}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{S^{\star}})^{2}}+\frac{\mu^{\star}}{K}\sum_{i\in E}\Big(\Psi^{\prime}_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}}+\frac{\ln T}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{i,\mathcal{R}})^{2}}+\Phi_{i,\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}}\Big)\right)

(453)

\displaystyle\cap\,

\displaystyle O\left(\sum_{i\in E}\frac{K\ln T}{\Delta_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B},\min}}+\frac{\lambda\mu^{\star}K^{2}\ln T}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{S^{\star}})^{2}}+K\mu^{\star}\sum_{i\in E}\Big(\Psi_{i,\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{B}}+\frac{\ln T}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{i,\mathcal{R}})^{2}}+\Phi_{i,\mathcal{S}^{c}\cap\mathcal{B}}\Big)\right)

(454)

where
\ln(1/\delta_{T})
in the
\Psi
and
\Psi^{\prime}
functions should be replaced by
\lambda\ln T
.

•

if
\ln(1/\delta_{T})\in\omega(\ln T)
, the regret
\mathrm{Reg}(T)
is

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}(T)\in\Omega\left(\frac{\mu^{\star}\ln(1/\delta_{T})}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{S^{\star}})^{2}}\right)\cap O\left(\frac{\mu^{\star}K^{2}\ln(1/\delta_{T})}{(\Delta^{\mathrm{v}}_{S^{\star}})^{2}}\right)

(455)

The upper bounds above are achieved by
PASCombUCB
.

Appendix E

Additional Discussions and Future Research

E.1

Discussions on the Tightness Results

In terms of the problem-dependent bounds in Corollary
4.4
, we consider general instances where the rewards from the items are independent and the gap in
\mathrm{Reg}_{1}(T)
can be closed when that the rewards from the items are correlated, as in the lower bound for the unconstrained combinatorial bandits in
Kveton et al. 2015
. This assumption also allows us to remove a factor of
K
from the gap of
\mathrm{Reg}_{2}(T)
.

In terms of the problem-independent bounds in Corollary
5.4
, the regret due to suboptimality is almost tight as in the unconstrained case
(
Kveton et al. 2015
)
. The regret due to safeness checking is tight up to a factor of
K^{2}
. During each phase,
PASCombUCB
selects and samples solutions which are disjoint subsets of
A_{p}
, and hence one item is sampled at most once during one phase. However, it is empirically feasible to sample some items more than once during one phase, which will help reduce the regret but requires more delicate analysis.

For future directions, it is of interest to close the gap (the factor
K
) in the regret due to safeness checking with improved analyses or additional assumptions on the instance.

E.2

Discussions on the Problem Formulation

Anytime safety
is important in safety-critical applications where
at each point in time
the risk cannot exceed a certain threshold. For example, in a self-driving car that is scheduled to move from start point
x_{0}
to end point
x_{n}
via
(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n-1})
(the choice of these waypoints is a combinatorial problem), it is necessary that the car stay in its designated lane at all points in time, and not just “on average”, otherwise a catastrophic accident might result at some point in time with non-negligible probability. In this example, we might want to choose a route that is safe at all times w.h.p. (in the sense that the car stays in its designated lane) at the cost of a longer travel time.

This work studies the anytime-safe constraint at the
super-arm
3
3

3

In this discussion, the terms “super arm” and “base arm” refer to “solution” and ”item” respectively.
level and provides a first step to understanding risk in combinatorial semi-bandits. The
sum

\sum_{i\in A}\sigma_{i}^{2}
of a set of items (base arms) in
A
is adopted as the risk measure, which is a certain function of
\sigma_{i}^{2},i\in A
.
It is also of interest to study the anytime safety at the
base arm level
, where the risk function is
\max_{i\in A}\sigma_{i}^{2}
. From a technical point of view, if the safeness of any single base arm has not been ascertained (as we need to learn the safeness/risk of the base arms), then pulling any base arm can be risky, in the sense that with non-vanishing probability, the anytime-safe constraint (or even the less stringent stagewise safety constraint
(
Khezeli & Bitar 2020
)
) is violated when we do the exploration (by pulling any base arm)
at the beginning
. Thus, this seems infeasible from a technical standpoint.
Nevertheless, we believe additional proper assumptions can be made to formulate a practical and feasible model that leads to future researches.

E.3

Comparisons

Comparison with
Wu et al. 2016
;
Kazerouni et al. 2017
:

While the conservative (linear) constraint

\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{t\geq 1}\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{t}\left\langle X_{k},\theta^{*}\right\rangle\geq(1-\alpha)tb_{0}\right\}\right]\geq 1-\delta

requires the constraint should be satisfied w.p.
1-\delta
over the whole horizon, which is similar to our probably anytime-safe constraint, the constraint is in terms of the
cumulative
expected reward (up to time step
t
). The cumulative nature of the conservative constraint maintains a “budget reservoir” that makes this constraint less stringent than the stagewise safety constraint
(
Khezeli & Bitar 2020
)
, in the sense that one algorithm may satisfy the conservative constraint but violate the stagewise safety constraint. Both the stagewise safety constraint and the probably anytime-safe constraint consider the reward/risk that is incurred at each
single
time step.

Comparison with
Khezeli & Bitar 2020
;
Moradipari et al. 2020
:

To the best of our knowledge, the
stagewise safety
(or the
stagewise conservative)
constraint
(
Khezeli & Bitar 2020
;
Moradipari et al. 2020
)
is the most related risk-aware constraint to our anytime-safe constraint.

(1) The stagewise conservative constraint is
a constraint on the mean reward
(hence, only one statistics is involved in the problem), which originates from the conservative constraint
(
Wu et al. 2016
)
. In our setup, we post the anytime-safe constraint on the risk while minimizing the regret, which requires us to consider
two statistics and the interaction between them
.

(2) The stagewise safety constraint has only been utilized under the linear bandit setup in the literature, where the arm set is assumed to be a
convex and compact
set in
\mathbb{R}^{n}

(
Moradipari et al. 2020
)
, and thus, the arms constitute an
uncountable continuous
set. If an arm
A
is known to be safe, then it is safe to pull any arm “near”
A
. However, in the combinatorial bandit setup, such a continuity property of the arm set does not hold since it is “discrete”. Specifically, given that super arm
A
is safe (but not absolutely safe), even the safeness of a nearby arm
\tilde{A}
, which is obtained by replacing one single base arm in
A
with another base arm, cannot be guaranteed by the safeness of
A
.

(3) In terms of the safety level, consider the stagewise safety constraint with a constant confidence parameter
\delta
(independent of
T
); intuitively, the safety constraint can be violated approximately
\delta T
times, which is
linear
in
T
. In addition, consider an algorithm which does safeness checking first, followed by exploration-and-exploitation on the safe super arms, it takes
\Theta(\frac{1}{(\Delta_{A}^{\mathrm{v}})^{2}}\ln\frac{1}{\delta})
pulls to identify the unsafeness of an unsafe super arm
A
. Note that the time required is independent of
T
, so the regret due to safeness checking is
o(T^{a})
for all
a>0
. From this perspective, the stagewise safety constraint is not stringent enough and can be easily satisfied by such a naive algorithm. A more direct intuition (yet not completely rigorous) is, if the algorithm ignores the stagewise safety constraint, it only takes
o(T^{a})
for all
a>0
to rule out the unsafe super arms, which indicates it satisfies the stagewise safety constraint (since the unsafe super arms are pulled
o(T^{a})<\delta T
times).
From another point of view, given a confidence parameter
\delta
, if the stagewise safety constraint is satisfied w.p.
1-\delta_{t}
at time step
t
with
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\delta_{t}=\delta
, then the probably anytime-safe constraint is met w.p.
1-\delta
.

(4) Besides the constant confidence parameter
\delta
, we have investigated the
whole spectrum
of
\delta
in terms of the time horizon
T
. The tightness result (Corollary
D.1
) indicates our algorithm is capable of dealing with
an even stricter constraint
(in the sense that
\delta
decreases with respect to
T
) and we provide a sharp threshold on the achievability of
o(T^{a})
(for all
a>0
) regret (see Theorem
4.5
).
